The **2025 New Jersey gubernatorial election** was held on November 4, 2025, to elect the [governor of New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_New_Jersey). [Democratic](5043544.md) U.S. representative [Mikie Sherrill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikie_Sherrill) defeated [Republican](32070.md) former nominee [Jack Ciattarelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Ciattarelli).[^cite_note-2][^cite_note-Landslide-3][^cite_note-4] Sherrill succeeded Democratic incumbent [Phil Murphy,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Murphy) who was [term-limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Term_limits_in_the_United_States) and thus ineligible to seek a third term.

[Primary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_elections) were held on June 10, 2025.[^cite_note-5] Sherrill won the Democratic nomination with 34% of the vote over a field of candidates that included Newark mayor [Ras Baraka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ras_Baraka) and Jersey City mayor [Steven Fulop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven_Fulop). Ciattarelli, the Republican nominee in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), won his second consecutive nomination with almost 68% of the vote over radio talk show host [Bill Spadea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Spadea).

After strong Republican performances in the state in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election) and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_New_Jersey), many analysts expected a close race. Some polls suggested the election was a toss-up.[^cite_note-AP_News-6][^cite_note-7] However, Sherrill outperformed expectations and won with a decisive 14.36% margin of victory, slightly larger than Murphy's 14.14% margin in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election). This election was the widest Democratic margin since [2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), and the highest vote share for a Democratic nominee since [1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election). Sherrill was the first Democratic gubernatorial candidate to win Morris County since [1973](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election). Sherrill also lost [Hunterdon County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunterdon_County,_New_Jersey) by single digits, which is the narrowest Republican margin since [1977](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election).[^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9] Both Sherrill and Ciattarelli obtained the most votes cast in any New Jersey gubernatorial election, and this election had the highest turnout since [1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election).[^cite_note-10]

Sherrill was sworn in as the 57th governor of New Jersey on January 20, 2026. She became the second [female governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_female_governors_in_the_United_States) in New Jersey history,[^cite_note-11] the first Democratic female governor in New Jersey history, and the first female military veteran to serve as governor of a U.S. state.[^cite_note-12] This election was the first New Jersey gubernatorial election since [1961](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1961_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election) in which either party won the governorship for three consecutive terms.[^cite_note-13][^cite_note-14]

## Background

[New Jersey](21648.md) has long been considered a [blue state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_state), consistently voting Democratic at the federal level since [1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_United_States_presidential_election) and generally voting Democratic at the state level as well. Republicans have not won a statewide election in New Jersey since the [2013 gubernatorial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), but recent statewide, especially gubernatorial, elections have been notably competitive.[^cite_note-15]

In [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), Governor Murphy was re-elected by a 3.2% margin,[^cite_note-16] considerably down from his 14.1% margin of victory in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election).[^cite_note-17] A similar rightward shift was seen at the presidential level in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_presidential_election_in_New_Jersey), when Democrat [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) won New Jersey by 5.9%,[^cite_note-18] considerably down from [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_presidential_election_in_New_Jersey), when [Joe Biden](145422.md) won New Jersey by 15.9%. Harris had the worst performance in New Jersey of any Democratic nominee since [1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_United_States_presidential_election_in_New_Jersey), with [Donald Trump](4848272.md) becoming the first Republican nominee to lose New Jersey by just single digits since [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_United_States_presidential_election_in_New_Jersey).[^cite_note-19]

Most polls, analysts and ratings showed Sherrill to be the favorite, although the polls narrowed beginning in mid-September.[^cite_note-AP_News-6]

This election was considered to be an indicator of the popularity of [Donald Trump's second presidency](78298108.md) in the state. Until [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), New Jersey had elected a governor of the opposite party of the sitting [president of the United States](24113.md) in every election since [1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election).[^cite_note-20] Until this election, New Jersey had not elected the same party to the governor's office for more than two consecutive terms since [1961](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1961_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election).[^cite_note-21]

In October 2025, the Trump administration announced that the [Department of Justice](52563.md) would deploy election monitors to polling sites in California and New Jersey ahead of the November 4 elections. The administration cited concerns raised by Republican officials about alleged voting irregularities. Democratic leaders in both states criticized the move as politically motivated [voter intimidation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_intimidation), while the department said the monitors would ensure transparency and compliance with federal law.[^cite_note-22]

### Lieutenant gubernatorial nomination

#### Nominee

- [Dale Caldwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dale_Caldwell), president of [Centenary University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centenary_University) (2023–present) and former [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey) school board member (1998–2024)

  [^cite_note-218]

#### Considered but not selected

- Samuel Delgado, vice chair of the New Jersey State Cannabis Regulatory Commission and husband of [state assemblymember](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_State_Assembly) [Yvonne Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yvonne_Lopez)

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

- [Shavar Jeffries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shavar_Jeffries), former assistant attorney general (2008–2010) and candidate for [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Newark,_New_Jersey) of [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey) in [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Newark_mayoral_election)

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

- [Jim Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Johnson_\(New_Jersey_politician\)), former [U.S. under secretary of the Treasury for Enforcement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Under_Secretary_of_the_Treasury_for_Terrorism_and_Financial_Intelligence) (1998–2001) and candidate for governor in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election)

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

- Carlos Medina, president of the Statewide Hispanic [Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamber_of_commerce) of New Jersey

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

- Lamont Repollet, president of [Kean University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kean_University) (2020–present) and former [New Jersey commissioner of education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Department_of_Education) (2018–2020)

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

#### Declined to be considered

- [Troy Singleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy_Singleton), [state senator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Senate) from the [7th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey's_7th_legislative_district) (2018–present)

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

- [Benjie Wimberly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjie_Wimberly), [state senator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Senate) from the [35th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey's_35th_legislative_district) (2025–present)

  [^cite_note-NJGlobe_Shortlist-219]

## Republican primary

### Candidates

#### Nominee

- [Jack Ciattarelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Ciattarelli), former [state assemblyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_General_Assembly) from the [16th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey's_16th_legislative_district) (2011–2018), nominee for [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_New_Jersey) in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), and candidate in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election)

  [^cite_note-220]

#### Eliminated in primary

## General election

The in-person [early voting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_voting) period ran from October 25 to November 2, 2025.[^cite_note-304]

### Candidates

- [Mikie Sherrill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikie_Sherrill) ([Democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Democratic_State_Committee)), [U.S. representative](19468510.md) from [New Jersey's 11th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey's_11th_congressional_district) (2019–2025)&#x20;

  - Running mate: [Dale Caldwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dale_Caldwell), president of [Centenary University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centenary_University) (2023–present) and former [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey) school board member (1998–2024)

- [Jack Ciattarelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Ciattarelli) ([Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Republican_State_Committee)), former [state assemblyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_General_Assembly) from the [16th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey's_16th_legislative_district) (2011–2018), nominee for [governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_New_Jersey) in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election), and candidate in [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_New_Jersey_gubernatorial_election)&#x20;

  - Running mate: [Jim Gannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Gannon_\(sheriff\)), [Morris County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morris_County,_New_Jersey) sheriff (2017–present)

- Vic Kaplan ([Libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_Party_of_New_Jersey)), former chair of the [New Jersey Libertarian Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Libertarian_Party) and nominee for [New Jersey's 12th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey's_12th_congressional_district) in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_New_Jersey#District_12)

  [^cite_note-305]

  - Running mate: Bruno Pereira, chair of the [New Jersey Libertarian Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Libertarian_Party)

- Joanne Kuniansky ([Socialist Workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Workers_Party_\(United_States\))), perennial candidate&#x20;

  - Running mate: Craig Honts
